3	## Phase 1 — Foundation (Current)

42	## Phase 3 — Metrics Engine
43	
44	Calculate raw stats and derived complex metrics from ingested data.
45	
46	Full metrics catalog: [`docs/features/metrics-catalog.md`](metrics-catalog.md)
47	GitHub API data reference: [`docs/integrations/github-api.md`](../integrations/github-api.md)
48	Jira API data reference: [`docs/integrations/jira-api.md`](../integrations/jira-api.md)
49	
50	### GitHub Raw Metrics (selected)
51	- [ ] Commit frequency, additions/deletions, after-hours rate, punch card patterns
52	- [ ] PR cycle time, size, draft rate, merge method distribution
53	- [ ] Review response time, review state distribution, thread resolution rate
54	- [ ] CI build success rate, build duration, flaky test rate
55	- [ ] Deployment frequency, lead time to production
56	- [ ] Security alert severity distribution, push_protection bypass tracking
57	
58	### Jira Raw Metrics (selected)
59	- [ ] Lead time, cycle time, time-in-status per ticket (from changelog)
60	- [ ] Story points committed vs. completed per sprint
61	- [ ] Estimation accuracy (`workratio`), re-pointing frequency
62	- [ ] Blocked time (Flagged/Impediment), handoff count, rework rate
63	- [ ] Sprint scope change, spillover, velocity trend
64	- [ ] WIP violations vs. board limits
65	
66	### Derived / Complex Metrics
67	- [ ] **Collaboration Score** — reviews + comments + cross-team participation
68	- [ ] **Mean Time to Development (MTTD)** — Jira created → first commit
69	- [ ] **Mean Time to Review (MTTR)** — PR opened → first review
70	- [ ] **Code Churn Rate** — lines rewritten within N days of writing
71	- [ ] **Burnout Risk Index** — after-hours + output variance + declining trend
72	- [ ] **Velocity Consistency Score** — stddev/mean of weekly output
73	- [ ] **Review Quality Score** — comment depth vs. rubber-stamp approvals
74	- [ ] **Impact Score** — weighted composite of all above
75	- [ ] **PR Health Score** — size + description + CI + review coverage
76	- [ ] **Team Health Index** — aggregate team-level composite
77	- [ ] **Estimation Accuracy Score** — per developer, per sprint, trended
78	- [ ] **Seniority Classification** — for global benchmark matching
79	
80	### Benchmarking Data
81	- [ ] Anonymized aggregation pipeline (B2B data)
82	- [ ] Public GitHub profile scraper for global benchmarks
83	- [ ] Seniority classification model (based on account age, repo stars, contribution history)
84	- [ ] Percentile calculation per metric per seniority band

1	# Product Overview
2	
3	> Working title: **no-name-proj** — name candidates: Apogee, Quasar, Nova, Pulsar, Aurora, Commit
4	
5	## What It Is
6	
7	B2B SaaS platform that tracks and analyzes developer and team performance using data from engineering tools — GitHub, GitLab, Jira, and more.
8	
9	It surfaces raw stats, calculates complex derived metrics, and uses AI to generate deep natural-language analysis, short actionable tips, and benchmark comparisons against teammates and global peers at the same seniority level.
10	
11	## Core Value Proposition
12	
13	Engineering managers and companies get objective, data-driven insight into:
14	- Individual developer output and growth over time
15	- Team health, velocity, and collaboration quality
16	- How their developers compare to global benchmarks at equivalent seniority
17	
18	## Target Users
19	
20	### B2B (Primary)
21	- **Engineering Managers** — visibility into team and individual performance
22	- **CTOs / VPs of Engineering** — org-wide performance trends, team comparisons
23	- **HR / People Ops** — objective performance data for reviews and promotions
24	- **Developers themselves** — personal dashboards, self-improvement tracking
25	
26	### B2C (Future)
27	- **Individual developers** — track personal stats independently, generate shareable performance cards (CV inserts, LinkedIn, GitHub README-style badges)
28	
29	## Target Company Size
30	
31	All segments: startups (10+), mid-market (50–500), enterprise (500+).
32	
33	## Data Sources
34	
35	### Phase 1
36	- **GitHub** — commits, PRs, reviews, code diff stats, cycle time, merge frequency
37	- **Jira** — tickets, sprints, velocity, estimation accuracy, issue resolution time
38	
39	### Future
40	- **GitLab** — same as GitHub
41	- **Linear**, **Shortcut**, **Azure DevOps** — project management alternatives
42	- **Slack** — collaboration signal (optional, privacy-sensitive)
43	
44	## Benchmark Data Sources
45	
46	Global seniority benchmarks are built from two sources:
47	1. **Anonymized B2B customer data** — aggregated across all companies on the platform
48	2. **Public GitHub profiles** — scraped open-source contribution data
49	
50	Both are anonymized and aggregated. No individual data is exposed externally.
51	
52	## Views & Comparison Modes
53	
54	| View | Description |
55	|------|-------------|
56	| Single developer | Full profile: all metrics, trends over time, AI analysis |
57	| Dev vs Dev | Side-by-side comparison of two developers |
58	| Dev vs Team | Individual measured against their team's average |
59	| Dev vs Global Benchmark | Individual measured against global peers at same seniority |
60	| Team overview | Aggregated team metrics, health indicators |
61	| Team vs Team | Cross-team comparison within a company |
62	| Over time | Any of the above with time-series view |
63	
64	## Presentation Formats
65	
66	- **Table** — raw stats, sortable, exportable
67	- **Charts & plots** — time series, bar, radar, heatmap
68	- **AI narrative** — deep natural-language analysis of performance patterns
69	- **Tips** — short actionable recommendations generated by AI
70	- **Shareable cards** — generated images for CVs, social media, GitHub README (B2C)
71	
72	## Metrics Philosophy
73	
74	Collect all possible raw data first. Derive complex metrics on top.
75	
76	### Raw Data (examples)
77	- Commits per day/week/month
78	- PR open/merge/cycle time
79	- Code review participation (given/received)
80	- Lines added/deleted/churned
81	- Ticket velocity, story points completed
82	- Sprint completion rate, estimation accuracy
83	- After-hours commit frequency
84	
85	### Derived / Complex Metrics (examples)
86	- **Collaboration Score** — composite of PR reviews, comments, pair activity
87	- **Mean Time to Development (MTTD)** — ticket → first commit
88	- **Mean Time to Review (MTTR)** — PR open → first review
89	- **Code Churn Rate** — rework ratio over time
90	- **Burnout Risk Index** — based on after-hours work, commit density spikes, declining output
91	- **Velocity Consistency** — variance in output week-over-week
92	- **Review Quality Score** — depth of review comments vs rubber-stamp approvals
93	- **Impact Score** — weighted composite of output, quality, collaboration
94	
95	## AI Layer
96	
97	- Deep natural-language performance analysis per developer/team
98	- Short actionable tips ("You take 40% longer on reviews than your team average")
99	- Anomaly detection ("Unusual drop in output last 2 weeks")
100	- Benchmark narratives ("You're in the top 20% of mid-level engineers globally")
101	- Natural language querying (future: "Who on my team is at burnout risk?")
102	
103	## Monetization
104	
105	Not defined yet. Focus is on building the product first.
106	
```

> AGENT

**Phase 2 — Data Ingestion.**

Phase 1 is complete. Without data, nothing in Phase 3–5 can be built or validated.

**Start with GitHub Integration** — it's the richer, more universal data source and has better webhook support than Jira. Specifically:

1. GitHub OAuth app setup (org-level)
2. Webhook ingestion (push, PR, review events)
3. Raw event storage (append-only)
4. Queue system (BullMQ) for async processing

Jira can follow in parallel once the pipeline pattern is established from GitHub.

Want to plan this out, or jump straight to implementation?

1	# Architecture Overview
2	
3	## System Design
4	
5	Monorepo with two independent workspaces:
6	
7	```
8	no-name-proj/
9	├── backend/     # NestJS v11 + Fastify API
10	├── frontend/    # React (not yet scaffolded)
11	└── docs/        # Documentation
12	```
13	
14	## High-Level Architecture
15	
16	```
17	┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
18	│                          Clients                                    │
19	│              Browser (Web App)  │  Future: Mobile App              │
20	└──────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────────────────┘
21	                       │ HTTPS
22	                       ▼
23	┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
24	│                       Backend API                                   │
25	│              NestJS v11 + Fastify                                   │
26	│                                                                     │
27	│  ┌─────────────┐  ┌──────────────┐  ┌────────────────────────────┐ │
28	│  │  Auth       │  │  Company /   │  │  Metrics / Analytics       │ │
29	│  │  Module     │  │  User Mgmt   │  │  Module (Phase 3+)         │ │
30	│  └─────────────┘  └──────────────┘  └────────────────────────────┘ │
31	│                                                                     │
32	│  ┌─────────────┐  ┌──────────────┐  ┌────────────────────────────┐ │
33	│  │  GitHub     │  │  Jira        │  │  AI / LLM                  │ │
34	│  │  Ingestion  │  │  Ingestion   │  │  Module (Phase 5+)         │ │
35	│  │  (Phase 2+) │  │  (Phase 2+)  │  │                            │ │
36	│  └─────────────┘  └──────────────┘  └────────────────────────────┘ │
37	└───────┬──────────────────┬──────────────────────┬───────────────────┘
38	        │                  │                      │
39	        ▼                  ▼                      ▼
40	┌──────────────┐  ┌─────────────────┐  ┌──────────────────────────┐
41	│  PostgreSQL  │  │  Redis          │  │  Job Queue               │
42	│              │  │                 │  │  (BullMQ, Phase 2+)      │
43	│  Read/Write  │  │  Read/Write     │  │                          │
44	│  Replicas    │  │  Separation     │  └──────────────────────────┘
45	│  via Knex    │  │  (ioredis/Keyv) │
46	└──────────────┘  └─────────────────┘
47	```
48	
49	## Backend Module Structure
50	
51	```
52	src/modules/
53	├── app/              # Root module
54	├── auth/             # OAuth (GitHub + Google), JWT, sessions, CSRF
55	├── user/             # User management
56	├── company/          # Company (tenant) management
57	├── company-member/   # Company membership, roles
58	├── invitation/       # Email invitations
59	├── health/           # Liveness + readiness probes
60	├── env-config/       # Environment config & validation
61	├── cache/            # Redis abstraction (read/write separation)
62	├── db/               # Knex database module
63	│   ├── users/
64	│   ├── companies/
65	│   ├── company-members/
66	│   ├── invitations/
67	│   └── oauth-accounts/
68	└── [future modules]
69	    ├── github/       # GitHub data ingestion (Phase 2)
70	    ├── jira/         # Jira data ingestion (Phase 2)
71	    ├── metrics/      # Metrics computation engine (Phase 3)
72	    ├── benchmarks/   # Global benchmark data (Phase 3)
73	    └── ai/           # AI analysis & tips (Phase 5)
74	```
75	
76	## Auth Flow
77	
78	```
79	Client                    Backend                    Redis           PostgreSQL
80	  │                          │                          │                │
81	  │  GET /auth/github        │                          │                │
82	  │─────────────────────────>│                          │                │
83	  │                          │  store PKCE verifier     │                │
84	  │                          │─────────────────────────>│                │
85	  │  redirect → GitHub OAuth │                          │                │
86	  │<─────────────────────────│                          │                │
87	  │                          │                          │                │
88	  │  GET /auth/github/callback?code=xxx                 │                │
89	  │─────────────────────────>│                          │                │
90	  │                          │  exchange code (Arctic)  │                │
91	  │                          │  store temp auth code    │                │
92	  │                          │─────────────────────────>│                │
93	  │  redirect → /auth/token?code=yyy                    │                │
94	  │<─────────────────────────│                          │                │
95	  │                          │                          │                │
96	  │  POST /auth/token {code} │                          │                │
97	  │─────────────────────────>│                          │                │
98	  │                          │  validate code           │                │
99	  │                          │─────────────────────────>│                │
100	  │                          │  upsert user/oauth       │                │
101	  │                          │──────────────────────────────────────────>│
102	  │                          │  store refresh token     │                │
103	  │                          │─────────────────────────>│                │
104	  │  {accessToken, refreshToken, csrfToken}             │                │
105	  │<─────────────────────────│                          │                │
106	```
107	
108	## Data Layer Patterns
109	
110	### Database
111	- **PostgreSQL** with read/write replica separation
112	- **Knex.js** (no ORM) via `nest-knexjs`
113	- Two connection tokens: `KNEX_READ_CONNECTION`, `KNEX_WRITE_CONNECTION`
114	- Model classes per table — single responsibility (queries only)
115	- Soft deletes via `deleted_at` column
116	
117	### Cache / Sessions
118	- **Redis** with read/write separation via `ioredis`
119	- **Keyv** abstraction layer with Redis adapter
120	- **Redlock** for distributed locking
121	- Namespaced cache keys per domain
122	- Server-side sessions — refresh token stored in Redis, validated on every request
123	- Refresh token theft detection via tombstone keys
124	
125	### Job Queue (Phase 2+)
126	- **BullMQ** for async ingestion jobs
127	- GitHub/Jira webhook events → queue → idempotent processor
128	- Historical backfill jobs
129	- Metrics recalculation jobs triggered by new data
130	
131	## Multi-Tenancy Model
132	
133	```
134	Company (Tenant)
135	    │
136	    ├── Admin (1 per company, DB-enforced)
137	    │
138	    └── Members (many)
139	            │
140	            └── CompanyMember record (role derived here, not on User)
141	                    │
142	                    └── User (can belong to multiple companies)
143	```
144	
145	- Roles: `admin` | `member`
146	- Admin is unique per company (partial unique index on `deleted_at IS NULL`)
147	- A user can be a member of multiple companies simultaneously
148	- Invitations: admin-only, email-based, role fixed to `member`, 72hr expiry
149	
150	## External Integrations (Phase 2+)
151	
152	### GitHub
153	- **OAuth App** for org-level access
154	- **Webhooks** for real-time events (push, PR, review, issue)
155	- **REST API** for historical backfill
156	- Rate limit handling — exponential backoff + queue throttling
157	
158	### Jira
159	- **OAuth 2.0** or API token
160	- **Webhooks** for real-time events
161	- **REST API** for historical backfill
162	- Jira Cloud + Server support consideration
163	
164	## AI Layer (Phase 5+)
165	
166	- LLM-based text generation (Claude API — Anthropic)
167	- Per-developer narrative generation from metrics snapshot
168	- Short tips generated from delta analysis (week-over-week changes)
169	- Anomaly detection signals fed as context to LLM
170	- Benchmark context injected into prompts for comparison narratives
171	
172	## Key Technical Decisions
173	
174	| Decision | Choice | Reason |
175	|----------|--------|--------|
176	| HTTP framework | Fastify | Performance over Express |
177	| ORM | None (Knex.js) | Full query control, no magic |
178	| Auth library | Arctic | PKCE OAuth2, lightweight |
179	| Sessions | Redis (server-side) | Security, revocability |
180	| Queue | BullMQ (planned) | Redis-native, battle-tested |
181	| AI provider | Anthropic Claude | Best reasoning, available API |
182	| DB | PostgreSQL | Multi-tenant, ACID, JSON support |
183	| Cache | Redis | Sessions, rate limiting, queues |
184	
```

6	
7	## Commits
8	
9	### Per Commit (single-commit endpoint only)

11	- `author` + `committer` (may differ on rebase/cherry-pick)
12	- `stats.additions`, `stats.deletions`, `stats.total`
13	- `parents[]` — count > 1 = merge commit

15	- `comment_count`
16	- Per file: `filename`, `status` (added/removed/modified/renamed/copied), `additions`, `deletions`, `patch`

19	- **`/stats/contributors`** — per contributor per week: `additions`, `deletions`, `commits` (52 weeks)
20	- **`/stats/commit_activity`** — per week total + per day of week (52 weeks)
21	- **`/stats/code_frequency`** — weekly additions/deletions since repo creation
22	- **`/stats/participation`** — last 52 weeks: all vs. owner commits
23	- **`/stats/punch_card`** — hourly distribution by day of week — work pattern analysis
24	
25	> **Note**: `additions`/`deletions` require N+1 calls on list endpoints. Use stats endpoints for aggregates.

30

43	- `mergeable`, `mergeable_state`, `rebaseable`

48	- `reviewThreads` — each thread: `isResolved`, `isOutdated`, `path`, `line`, `resolvedBy`
49	- `timelineItems` — full ordered event stream (labels, assignments, reviews, commits, status changes)
50	- `closingIssuesReferences` — issues that close on merge
51	- `participants` — all users who touched the PR
52	- `suggestedReviewers` — ML-recommended reviewers

54	- `totalCommentsCount` — combined count

First question:

Can a company have **multiple GitHub organizations** connected (e.g. a holding company with 2 GitHub orgs), or is it always **one GitHub org per company**?

> DEVELOPER

> AGENT

> AGENT

Good, I have enough to propose approaches.

---

## Three approaches for GitHub org access

### Option A — GitHub App (recommended)

Admin installs a **GitHub App** on their GitHub org. The app gets an installation token scoped to that org. You can request exactly the permissions you need (repos, PRs, webhooks, etc.).

**Pros:** Granular permissions, works on private repos, installation tokens auto-rotate (1hr), webhooks are configured per-install, GitHub's recommended path for org-level tooling, supports multiple orgs cleanly.

**Cons:** Slightly more setup — you register one GitHub App under your developer account, admins install it on their org.

---

### Option B — GitHub OAuth App

Admin does an OAuth flow granting broad scopes (`repo`, `read:org`). You store their OAuth token and use it to call the API as them.

**Pros:** Simpler, no GitHub App registration needed, familiar OAuth pattern (you already use it for login).

**Cons:** Token is tied to one person's account — if they leave the company, access breaks. Scopes are coarse. No built-in webhook management. Rate limits tied to one user (5000 req/hr vs. 15000 for GitHub Apps).

---

### Option C — Personal Access Token

Admin generates a PAT and pastes it into your app.

**Pros:** Zero OAuth complexity.

**Cons:** Fragile (manually rotated), security risk (broad access), terrible UX. Rule this out.

---

**My recommendation: Option A — GitHub App.**

It's the right architecture for a B2B product. Token rotation is automatic, rate limits are higher, permissions are explicit, and multi-org support is clean (each org gets its own installation). The install UX is a standard GitHub flow — admin clicks "Install App", picks their org, and you're done.

Does this direction work for you?

> DEVELOPER

ok go plan A

## Design — Section 1: Installation Flow

Here's how the end-to-end connection works:

```
Admin (browser)              Backend                     GitHub
      │                         │                           │
      │  POST /integrations/    │                           │
      │  github/connect         │                           │
      │────────────────────────>│                           │
      │                         │  generate state token     │
      │                         │  store in Redis (5min)    │
      │  redirect to GitHub     │                           │
      │<────────────────────────│                           │
      │                         │                           │
      │  Install app on org ────────────────────────────────>
      │  (picks repos)          │                           │
      │                         │                           │
      │  GET /integrations/github/callback                  │
      │  ?installation_id=xxx&state=yyy                     │
      │────────────────────────>│                           │
      │                         │  validate state           │
      │                         │  GET /app/installations/  │
      │                         │  {id} ───────────────────>│
      │                         │  store installation       │
      │                         │  in DB                    │
      │  redirect → /settings   │                           │
      │<────────────────────────│                           │
```

**State token** is a short-lived Redis key scoped to `company_id + user_id` — prevents CSRF and ties the installation back to the right tenant.

Does this flow look right?

> DEVELOPER

ok... do they need to do it only once? and we will have acces via API to some data in GH?

> AGENT

Yes to both.

**Install once per org.** After the admin installs the GitHub App on their org, that's it. The installation persists until they uninstall it or revoke access from GitHub settings. No re-auth needed — tokens are generated server-side from the installation using your app's private key.

**Full API access after install.** With a valid installation, you can call GitHub's REST + GraphQL APIs as that installation — read repos, commits, PRs, reviews, contributors, etc. All private repos included (scoped to whatever repos they selected during install). Rate limit is 15,000 req/hr per installation.

You also get **webhooks for free** — GitHub App webhooks are configured once at the app level. Every installation automatically forwards events (push, PR, review, etc.) to your single webhook endpoint. You identify which org it came from via the `installation.id` in the payload.

---

Ready to continue to **Section 2: Data model** (what we store in the DB for installations)? Or any questions first?

> DEVELOPER

yep

> AGENT

## Design — Section 2: Data Model

Two new tables:

**`github_installations`** — one row per org connected to a company

| column | type | notes |
|---|---|---|
| `id` | uuid PK | |
| `company_id` | uuid FK | → companies |
| `installation_id` | bigint unique | GitHub's ID |
| `org_login` | varchar | e.g. `acme-corp` |
| `org_id` | bigint | GitHub's org ID |
| `status` | enum | `active` \| `suspended` \| `uninstalled` |
| `installed_at` | timestamptz | |
| `suspended_at` | timestamptz nullable | |
| `uninstalled_at` | timestamptz nullable | |
| `created_at` / `updated_at` | timestamptz | |

No `deleted_at` — uninstalls are a state change, not a soft delete. We keep the row for audit.

**`github_repos`** — repos we have access to under an installation

| column | type | notes |
|---|---|---|
| `id` | uuid PK | |
| `installation_id` | uuid FK | → github_installations |
| `github_repo_id` | bigint unique | GitHub's repo ID |
| `name` | varchar | `repo-name` |
| `full_name` | varchar | `org/repo-name` |
| `private` | boolean | |
| `default_branch` | varchar | |
| `synced_at` | timestamptz nullable | last successful data pull |
| `created_at` / `updated_at` | timestamptz | |

We populate `github_repos` immediately after install by calling `GET /installation/repositories`.

---

Does this model look right, or do you need anything else tracked at this level?

> DEVELOPER

ok ok

> AGENT

## Design — Section 3: Token Management

GitHub App installation tokens expire after **1 hour**. We never store them permanently — we generate them on demand and cache them in Redis.

**Flow:**
```
Request needs GitHub API access
        │
        ▼
Check Redis: github:token:{installation_id}
        │
   hit  │  miss
        │    │
        │    ▼
        │  Sign JWT with app private key
        │  POST /app/installations/{id}/access_tokens → GitHub
        │  Store token in Redis (TTL: 55min)
        │
        ▼
Use token for API call
```

**Why 55min TTL?** Tokens last 60min — we expire our cache 5min early to avoid using a token that's about to expire mid-request.

The private key lives in env config (already have `env-config` module). Never touches the DB.

---

## Design — Section 4: Webhook Handling

One endpoint: `POST /integrations/github/webhook`

GitHub signs every payload with an HMAC-SHA256 signature using your webhook secret. We verify it before processing.

```
Webhook arrives
      │
      ▼
Verify X-Hub-Signature-256
      │
   invalid → 401
      │
   valid
      │
      ▼
Extract installation_id from payload
Lookup github_installations record
      │
      ▼
Push raw event → BullMQ queue
      │
      ▼
Return 200 immediately
```

We return 200 fast and process async. GitHub will retry if we don't respond within 10 seconds.

---

Does sections 3 and 4 look good?

> DEVELOPER

ok ok

{
  "file_path": "/Users/4thwithme/Documents/repo/no-name-proj/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-04-14-github-app-integration-design.md",
  "content": "# GitHub App Integration — Design Spec\n\n**Date:** 2026-04-14\n**Phase:** 2 — Data Ingestion\n**Scope:** How companies connect their GitHub organization(s) to the platform\n\n---\n\n## Overview\n\nAdmins connect their GitHub organization to the platform by installing a GitHub App. Installation is a one-time action per org. After install, the backend has persistent API access to all private repos in that org and receives real-time webhook events automatically.\n\nA company may have multiple GitHub orgs connected (multiple installations), but most will have one.\n\n---\n\n## Approach: GitHub App\n\nWe register **one GitHub App** under our developer account. Admins install it on their GitHub org via a standard GitHub install flow. We use the installation ID + our app's private key to generate short-lived API tokens server-side — no user token needed after initial install.\n\n**Why not OAuth App:** OAuth tokens are tied to one person's account. If that person leaves, access breaks. Scopes are coarse, rate limits lower (5k vs 15k req/hr), no built-in webhook management.\n\n**Why not PAT:** Fragile, manually rotated, poor UX, security risk.\n\n---\n\n## Installation Flow\n\n```\nAdmin (browser)              Backend                     GitHub\n      │                         │                           │\n      │  POST /integrations/    │                           │\n      │  github/connect         │                           │\n      │────────────────────────>│                           │\n      │                         │  generate state token     │\n      │                         │  store in Redis (5min)    │\n      │  redirect to GitHub     │                           │\n      │<────────────────────────│                           │\n      │                         │                           │\n      │  Install app on org ────────────────────────────────>\n      │  (selects repos)        │                           │\n      │                         │                           │\n      │  GET /integrations/github/callback                  │\n      │  ?installation_id=xxx&state=yyy                     │\n      │────────────────────────>│                           │\n      │                         │  validate state           │\n      │                         │  GET /app/installations/  │\n      │                         │  {id} ───────────────────>│\n      │                         │  store installation in DB │\n      │                         │  fetch + store repos      │\n      │  redirect → /settings   │                           │\n      │<────────────────────────│                           │\n```\n\n**State token:** Short-lived Redis key (5min TTL), keyed by `company_id + user_id`. Prevents CSRF and ties the GitHub callback back to the correct tenant.\n\n**Endpoint summary:**\n- `POST /integrations/github/connect` — initiates install, returns redirect URL\n- `GET /integrations/github/callback` — GitHub redirects here after install\n- `DELETE /integrations/github/installations/:id` — admin disconnects an org\n\n---\n\n## Data Model\n\n### `github_installations`\n\nOne row per GitHub org connected to a company.\n\n| column | type | notes |\n|---|---|---|\n| `id` | uuid PK | |\n| `company_id` | uuid FK | → companies |\n| `installation_id` | bigint unique | GitHub's installation ID |\n| `org_login` | varchar | e.g. `acme-corp` |\n| `org_id` | bigint | GitHub's org ID |\n| `status` | enum | `active` \\| `suspended` \\| `uninstalled` |\n| `installed_at` | timestamptz | |\n| `suspended_at` | timestamptz nullable | set when GitHub sends suspend event |\n| `uninstalled_at` | timestamptz nullable | set when GitHub sends delete event |\n| `created_at` | timestamptz | |\n| `updated_at` | timestamptz | |\n\nNo `deleted_at` — status changes are state transitions, not soft deletes. Rows are kept for audit.\n\n### `github_repos`\n\nOne row per repo accessible under an installation.\n\n| column | type | notes |\n|---|---|---|\n| `id` | uuid PK | |\n| `installation_id` | uuid FK | → github_installations |\n| `github_repo_id` | bigint unique | GitHub's repo ID |\n| `name` | varchar | e.g. `api` |\n| `full_name` | varchar | e.g. `acme-corp/api` |\n| `private` | boolean | |\n| `default_branch` | varchar | |\n| `synced_at` | timestamptz nullable | last successful data pull |\n| `created_at` | timestamptz | |\n| `updated_at` | timestamptz | |\n\nPopulated immediately after install via `GET /installation/repositories`. Updated when GitHub sends `installation_repositories` webhook events (repos added/removed).\n\n---\n\n## Token Management\n\nInstallation access tokens expire after **1 hour**. Never stored permanently — generated on demand and cached in Redis.\n\n```\nNeed GitHub API token for installation_id X\n        │\n        ▼\nCheck Redis: github:token:{installation_id}\n        │\n   HIT  │  MISS\n        │    │\n        │    ▼\n        │  Sign JWT (RS256) with app private key\n        │  POST /app/installations/{id}/access_tokens\n        │  Cache token in Redis TTL=55min\n        │\n        ▼\nUse token for API call\n```\n\n**55min TTL:** Tokens are valid 60min. We expire cache 5min early to avoid mid-request expiry.\n\n**Private key:** Stored in env config (`ENV_CONFIG` module). Never written to the database.\n\n---\n\n## Webhook Handling\n\nGitHub App webhooks are configured once at the app level. Every installation automatically forwards events to our single webhook endpoint.\n\n**Endpoint:** `POST /integrations/github/webhook`\n\n**Processing:**\n```\nWebhook arrives\n      │\n      ▼\nVerify X-Hub-Signature-256 (HMAC-SHA256 with webhook secret)\n      │\n   invalid → 401\n      │\n   valid\n      │\n      ▼\nExtract installation_id from payload\nLookup github_installations — reject if not found or not active\n      │\n      ▼\nPush raw event to BullMQ queue (topic: github.raw_event)\n      │\n      ▼\nReturn 200 immediately\n```\n\nWe respond within GitHub's 10-second timeout. All processing is async via BullMQ.\n\n**Events handled (initial scope):**\n- `push` — new commits\n- `pull_request` — opened, closed, merged, synchronize\n- `pull_request_review` — submitted\n- `pull_request_review_comment` — created\n- `installation` — created, deleted, suspend, unsuspend\n- `installation_repositories` — added, removed\n\n**Raw event storage:** All events are stored as-is (append-only) before processing. This enables replays and historical debugging.\n\n---\n\n## GitHub App Configuration (one-time setup)\n\nRegistered once under the platform's GitHub account:\n\n- **Webhook URL:** `https://api.{domain}/integrations/github/webhook`\n- **Webhook secret:** stored in env config\n- **Permissions required:**\n  - Repository: Contents (read), Pull requests (read), Commit statuses (read), Checks (read)\n  - Organization: Members (read)\n  - Metadata: read (mandatory)\n- **Subscribe to events:** push, pull_request, pull_request_review, pull_request_review_comment, installation, installation_repositories\n\n---\n\n## Error Handling\n\n| Scenario | Handling |\n|---|---|\n| State mismatch on callback | 400, log, abort |\n| GitHub API down during callback | 500, admin sees error, can retry |\n| Installation not found in DB | Webhook returns 200 (silent drop), alert logged |\n| Suspended installation | Webhook events dropped until unsuspended |\n| Token generation failure | Retry 3x with backoff, then fail the job |\n| Repo sync failure after install | Retry via BullMQ job, admin sees \"syncing\" state |\n\n---\n\n## Module Structure\n\nNew NestJS module: `src/modules/github/`\n\n```\ngithub/\n├── github.module.ts\n├── github-app.service.ts        # token generation, API client\n├── github-webhook.service.ts    # webhook verification + routing\n├── github-install.service.ts    # installation connect/disconnect flow\n├── github-install.controller.ts # /integrations/github/* endpoints\n├── github-webhook.controller.ts # /integrations/github/webhook\n└── db/\n    ├── github-installations.model.ts\n    └── github-repos.model.ts\n```\n\nFollows the existing module pattern in the codebase.\n"
}
```

1	# GitHub App Integration — Design Spec
2	
3	**Date:** 2026-04-14
4	**Phase:** 2 — Data Ingestion
5	**Scope:** How companies connect their GitHub organization(s) to the platform
6	
7	---
8	
9	## Overview
10	
11	Admins connect their GitHub organization to the platform by installing a GitHub App. Installation is a one-time action per org. After install, the backend has persistent API access to all private repos in that org and receives real-time webhook events automatically.
12	
13	A company may have multiple GitHub orgs connected (multiple installations), but most will have one.
14	
15	---
16	
17	## Approach: GitHub App
18	
19	We register **one GitHub App** under our developer account. Admins install it on their GitHub org via a standard GitHub install flow. We use the installation ID + our app's private key to generate short-lived API tokens server-side — no user token needed after initial install.
20	
21	**Why not OAuth App:** OAuth tokens are tied to one person's account. If that person leaves, access breaks. Scopes are coarse, rate limits lower (5k vs 15k req/hr), no built-in webhook management.
22	
23	**Why not PAT:** Fragile, manually rotated, poor UX, security risk.
24	
25	---
26	
27	## Installation Flow
28	
29	```
30	Admin (browser)              Backend                     GitHub
31	      │                         │                           │
32	      │  POST /integrations/    │                           │
33	      │  github/connect         │                           │
34	      │────────────────────────>│                           │
35	      │                         │  generate state token     │
36	      │                         │  store in Redis (5min)    │
37	      │  redirect to GitHub     │                           │
38	      │<────────────────────────│                           │
39	      │                         │                           │
40	      │  Install app on org ────────────────────────────────>
41	      │  (selects repos)        │                           │
42	      │                         │                           │
43	      │  GET /integrations/github/callback                  │
44	      │  ?installation_id=xxx&state=yyy                     │
45	      │────────────────────────>│                           │
46	      │                         │  validate state           │
47	      │                         │  GET /app/installations/  │
48	      │                         │  {id} ───────────────────>│
49	      │                         │  store installation in DB │
50	      │                         │  fetch + store repos      │
51	      │  redirect → /settings   │                           │
52	      │<────────────────────────│                           │
53	```
54	
55	**State token:** Short-lived Redis key (5min TTL), keyed by `company_id + user_id`. Prevents CSRF and ties the GitHub callback back to the correct tenant.
56	
57	**Endpoint summary:**
58	- `POST /integrations/github/connect` — initiates install, returns redirect URL
59	- `GET /integrations/github/callback` — GitHub redirects here after install
60	- `DELETE /integrations/github/installations/:id` — admin disconnects an org
61	
62	---
63	
64	## Data Model
65	
66	### `github_installations`
67	
68	One row per GitHub org connected to a company.
69	
70	| column | type | notes |
71	|---|---|---|
72	| `id` | uuid PK | |
73	| `company_id` | uuid FK | → companies |
74	| `installation_id` | bigint unique | GitHub's installation ID |
75	| `org_login` | varchar | e.g. `acme-corp` |
76	| `org_id` | bigint | GitHub's org ID |
77	| `status` | enum | `active` \| `suspended` \| `uninstalled` |
78	| `installed_at` | timestamptz | |
79	| `suspended_at` | timestamptz nullable | set when GitHub sends suspend event |
80	| `uninstalled_at` | timestamptz nullable | set when GitHub sends delete event |
81	| `created_at` | timestamptz | |
82	| `updated_at` | timestamptz | |
83	
84	No `deleted_at` — status changes are state transitions, not soft deletes. Rows are kept for audit.
85	
86	### `github_repos`
87	
88	One row per repo accessible under an installation.
89	
90	| column | type | notes |
91	|---|---|---|
92	| `id` | uuid PK | |
93	| `installation_id` | uuid FK | → github_installations |
94	| `github_repo_id` | bigint unique | GitHub's repo ID |
95	| `name` | varchar | e.g. `api` |
96	| `full_name` | varchar | e.g. `acme-corp/api` |
97	| `private` | boolean | |
98	| `default_branch` | varchar | |
99	| `synced_at` | timestamptz nullable | last successful data pull |
100	| `created_at` | timestamptz | |
101	| `updated_at` | timestamptz | |
102	
103	Populated immediately after install via `GET /installation/repositories`. Updated when GitHub sends `installation_repositories` webhook events (repos added/removed).
104	
105	---
106	
107	## Token Management
108	
109	Installation access tokens expire after **1 hour**. Never stored permanently — generated on demand and cached in Redis.
110	
111	```
112	Need GitHub API token for installation_id X
113	        │
114	        ▼
115	Check Redis: github:token:{installation_id}
116	        │
117	   HIT  │  MISS
118	        │    │
119	        │    ▼
120	        │  Sign JWT (RS256) with app private key
121	        │  POST /app/installations/{id}/access_tokens
122	        │  Cache token in Redis TTL=55min
123	        │
124	        ▼
125	Use token for API call
126	```
127	
128	**55min TTL:** Tokens are valid 60min. We expire cache 5min early to avoid mid-request expiry.
129	
130	**Private key:** Stored in env config (`ENV_CONFIG` module). Never written to the database.
131	
132	---
133	
134	## Webhook Handling
135	
136	GitHub App webhooks are configured once at the app level. Every installation automatically forwards events to our single webhook endpoint.
137	
138	**Endpoint:** `POST /integrations/github/webhook`
139	
140	**Processing:**
141	```
142	Webhook arrives
143	      │
144	      ▼
145	Verify X-Hub-Signature-256 (HMAC-SHA256 with webhook secret)
146	      │
147	   invalid → 401
148	      │
149	   valid
150	      │
151	      ▼
152	Extract installation_id from payload
153	Lookup github_installations — reject if not found or not active
154	      │
155	      ▼
156	Push raw event to BullMQ queue (topic: github.raw_event)
157	      │
158	      ▼
159	Return 200 immediately
160	```
161	
162	We respond within GitHub's 10-second timeout. All processing is async via BullMQ.
163	
164	**Events handled (initial scope):**
165	- `push` — new commits
166	- `pull_request` — opened, closed, merged, synchronize
167	- `pull_request_review` — submitted
168	- `pull_request_review_comment` — created
169	- `installation` — created, deleted, suspend, unsuspend
170	- `installation_repositories` — added, removed
171	
172	**Raw event storage:** All events are stored as-is (append-only) before processing. This enables replays and historical debugging.
173	
174	---
175	
176	## GitHub App Configuration (one-time setup)
177	
178	Registered once under the platform's GitHub account:
179	
180	- **Webhook URL:** `https://api.{domain}/integrations/github/webhook`
181	- **Webhook secret:** stored in env config
182	- **Permissions required:**
183	  - Repository: Contents (read), Pull requests (read), Commit statuses (read), Checks (read)
184	  - Organization: Members (read)
185	  - Metadata: read (mandatory)
186	- **Subscribe to events:** push, pull_request, pull_request_review, pull_request_review_comment, installation, installation_repositories
187	
188	---
189	
190	## Error Handling
191	
192	| Scenario | Handling |
193	|---|---|
194	| State mismatch on callback | 400, log, abort |
195	| GitHub API down during callback | 500, admin sees error, can retry |
196	| Installation not found in DB | Webhook returns 200 (silent drop), alert logged |
197	| Suspended installation | Webhook events dropped until unsuspended |
198	| Token generation failure | Retry 3x with backoff, then fail the job |
199	| Repo sync failure after install | Retry via BullMQ job, admin sees "syncing" state |
200	
201	---
202	
203	## Module Structure
204	
205	New NestJS module: `src/modules/github/`
206	
207	```
208	github/
209	├── github.module.ts
210	├── github-app.service.ts        # token generation, API client
211	├── github-webhook.service.ts    # webhook verification + routing
212	├── github-install.service.ts    # installation connect/disconnect flow
213	├── github-install.controller.ts # /integrations/github/* endpoints
214	├── github-webhook.controller.ts # /integrations/github/webhook
215	└── db/
216	    ├── github-installations.model.ts
217	    └── github-repos.model.ts
218	```
219	
220	Follows the existing module pattern in the codebase.
221	
```

---
import { Injectable, Logger } from '@nestjs/common';
import { ConfigService } from '@nestjs/config';

@Injectable()
export class EnvConfigService {
	private readonly logger = new Logger(EnvConfigService.name);

	constructor(private readonly configService: ConfigService) {}

	get<T = unknown>({
		key,
		converter,
	}: {
		key: string;
		converter?: (param: unknown) => T;
	}): T {
		this.logger.log(`Fetching env var: ${key}`);

		const envVariable = this.configService.get<T>(key);

		if (envVariable === undefined) {
			throw new Error(`Environment variable ${key} is not defined`);
		}

		return converter ? converter(envVariable) : envVariable;
	}
}
```

1	import { randomUUID } from 'node:crypto';
2	
3	import { Injectable, Logger } from '@nestjs/common';
4	import { plainToInstance } from 'class-transformer';
5	import { validate, ValidationError } from 'class-validator';
6	import { Knex } from 'knex';
7	import { InjectConnection } from 'nest-knexjs';
8	
9	import { CompanyBaseDto } from '@modules/company/dto/company.base.dto';
10	import { ICompanyBase } from '@modules/company/types/company.base.interface';
11	import { ICompanyCreate } from '@modules/company/types/company.create.interface';
12	import { KNEX_READ_CONNECTION, KNEX_WRITE_CONNECTION } from '@modules/db/db.constant';
13	
14	import { TABLE_NAME_COMPANIES } from '@constants/db.constant';
15	
16	@Injectable()
17	export class CompanyModel {
18		private readonly logger = new Logger(CompanyModel.name);
19		private readonly tableName = TABLE_NAME_COMPANIES;
20	
21		constructor(
22			@InjectConnection(KNEX_READ_CONNECTION) private readonly knexRead: Knex,
23			@InjectConnection(KNEX_WRITE_CONNECTION) private readonly knexWrite: Knex,
24		) {}
25	
26		async findById({
27			companyId,
28		}: {
29			companyId: ICompanyBase['_id'];
30		}): Promise<ICompanyBase | null> {
31			try {
32				const result = await this.knexRead<ICompanyBase>(this.tableName)
33					.where({ _id: companyId })
34					.whereNull('deleted_at')
35					.first();
36	
37				if (!result) {
38					return null;
39				}
40	
41				const dto = plainToInstance(CompanyBaseDto, result);
42				const errors = await validate(dto, { skipMissingProperties: true });
43				if (errors.length > 0) {
44					this.logger.error(
45						`Validation failed for company data: ${this.formatValidationErrors({ errors })}`,
46					);
47					throw new Error(`Validation failed: ${this.formatValidationErrors({ errors })}`);
48				}
49	
50				return result;
51			} catch (error) {
52				this.logger.error(
53					`Failed to find company by id ${companyId}: ${(error as Error).message}`,
54				);
55				throw error;
56			}
57		}
58	
59		async create({ data }: { data: ICompanyCreate }): Promise<ICompanyBase> {
60			try {
61				const dto = plainToInstance(CompanyBaseDto, data);
62				const errors = await validate(dto, { skipMissingProperties: true });
63				if (errors.length > 0) {
64					this.logger.error(
65						`Validation failed for company data: ${this.formatValidationErrors({ errors })}`,
66					);
67					throw new Error(`Validation failed: ${this.formatValidationErrors({ errors })}`);
68				}
69	
70				const _id = randomUUID();
71				const [created] = await this.knexWrite<ICompanyBase>(this.tableName)
72					.insert({
73						_id,
74						...data,
75						created_at: this.knexWrite.fn.now(),
76						updated_at: this.knexWrite.fn.now(),
77					})
78					.returning('*');
79	
80				if (!created) {
81					throw new Error('Failed to create company');
82				}
83	
84				this.logger.debug(`Created company with id ${_id}`);
85				return created;
86			} catch (error) {
87				this.logger.error(`Failed to create company: ${(error as Error).message}`);
88				throw error;
89			}
90		}
91	
92		async softDelete({ companyId }: { companyId: ICompanyBase['_id'] }): Promise<void> {
93			try {
94				await this.knexWrite<ICompanyBase>(this.tableName)
95					.where({ _id: companyId })
96					.whereNull('deleted_at')
97					.update({
98						deleted_at: this.knexWrite.fn.now(),
99						updated_at: this.knexWrite.fn.now(),
100					});
101	
102				this.logger.debug(`Soft deleted company ${companyId}`);
103			} catch (error) {
104				this.logger.error(
105					`Failed to soft delete company ${companyId}: ${(error as Error).message}`,
106				);
107				throw error;
108			}
109		}
110	
111		private formatValidationErrors({ errors }: { errors: ValidationError[] }): string {
112			return errors
113				.map(
114					(error) =>
115						`${error.property}: ${Object.values(error.constraints ?? {}).join(', ')}`,
116				)
117				.join('; ');
118		}
119	}
120

1	import { Logger } from '@nestjs/common';
2	
3	import { TABLE_NAME_COMPANIES } from '@constants/db.constant';
4	
5	import type { Knex } from 'knex';
6	
7	const logger = new Logger('20260322120001_create_companies_table.ts');
8	
9	export async function up(knex: Knex): Promise<void> {
10		logger.log('Creating companies table');
11	
12		await knex.transaction(async (trx) => {
13			const tableExists = await trx.schema.hasTable(TABLE_NAME_COMPANIES);
14	
15			if (!tableExists) {
16				await trx.schema.createTable(TABLE_NAME_COMPANIES, (table) => {
17					table.uuid('_id').primary().notNullable();
18					table.string('name', 255).notNullable();
19					table
20						.timestamp('created_at', { useTz: true })
21						.defaultTo(trx.fn.now())
22						.notNullable();
23					table
24						.timestamp('updated_at', { useTz: true })
25						.defaultTo(trx.fn.now())
26						.notNullable();
27					table.timestamp('deleted_at', { useTz: true }).nullable();
28				});
29	
30				logger.log('Successfully created companies table');
31			} else {
32				logger.log('companies table already exists, skipping');
33			}
34		});
35	}
36	
37	export async function down(knex: Knex): Promise<void> {
38		logger.log('Dropping companies table');
39	
40		await knex.transaction(async (trx) => {
41			await trx.schema.dropTableIfExists(TABLE_NAME_COMPANIES);
42		});
43	}
44

11	import { CurrentUser } from '@modules/auth/decorators/current-user.decorator';
12	import { IRequestUser } from '@modules/auth/types/auth.interface';
13	import { CompanyService } from '@modules/company/company.service';
14	import { ICompanyBase } from '@modules/company/types/company.base.interface';
15	import { CompanyMemberService } from '@modules/company-member/company-member.service';
16	
17	import { LogExecution } from '@decorators/log-execution.decorator';

19	@ApiTags('Companies')
20	@Controller('companies')
21	export class CompanyController {
22		constructor(
23			private readonly companyService: CompanyService,
24			private readonly companyMemberService: CompanyMemberService,
25		) {}
26	
27		@Get(':id')
28		@ApiOperation({ summary: 'Get company by ID' })
29		@ApiResponse({ status: HttpStatus.OK, description: 'Company details' })
30		@ApiResponse({ status: HttpStatus.FORBIDDEN, description: 'User is not a member' })
31		@ApiResponse({ status: HttpStatus.NOT_FOUND, description: 'Company not found' })
32		@LogExecution()
33		async getById(
34			@Param('id') id: string,
35			@CurrentUser() user: IRequestUser,
36		): Promise<ICompanyBase> {
37			const membership = await this.companyMemberService.findByUserAndCompany({
38				userId: user.user_id,
39				companyId: id,
40			});
41	
42			if (!membership) throw new ForbiddenException('User is not a member of this company');
43	
44			const company = await this.companyService.findById({ companyId: id });
45	
46			if (!company) throw new NotFoundException('Company not found');
47	
48			return company;
49		}
50	}
51

import { Logger } from '@nestjs/common';
import { Transform, plainToInstance } from 'class-transformer';
import {
	IsBoolean,
	IsEnum,
	IsNotEmpty,
	IsNumber,
	IsString,
	Length,
	Max,
	Min,
	MinLength,
	validateSync,
} from 'class-validator';

import { Environment } from '@interfaces/env-variables.interface';

class EnvironmentVariables {
	@IsEnum(Environment)
	NODE_ENV: Environment;

	@IsNumber()
	@Min(0)
	@Max(65535)
	PORT: number;

	@IsString()
	@IsNotEmpty()
	GLOBAL_PREFIX: string;

	@IsString()
	@IsNotEmpty()
	SERVER_NAME: string;

	@IsBoolean()
	IS_COMPACT_LOGS: boolean;

	@IsBoolean()
	IS_JSON_LIKE_LOGS: boolean;

	@IsNumber()
	@Min(0)
	@Max(60000)
	GRACEFUL_SHUTDOWN_DELAY: number;

	@IsString()
	@IsNotEmpty()
	DATABASE_URL: string;

	@IsString()
	@IsNotEmpty()
	DATABASE_URL_READ_REPLICA: string;

	@IsString()
	@IsNotEmpty()
	REDIS_URL: string;

	@IsString()
	@IsNotEmpty()
	REDIS_READ_URL: string;

	@IsString()
	@MinLength(32)
	JWT_SECRET: string;

	@Transform(({ value }) => Number(value))
	@IsNumber()
	@Min(60)
	JWT_ACCESS_TOKEN_EXPIRY: number;

	@Transform(({ value }) => Number(value))
	@IsNumber()
	@Min(1)
	JWT_REFRESH_TOKEN_EXPIRY_DAYS: number;

	@IsString()
	@IsNotEmpty()
	GITHUB_CLIENT_ID: string;

	@IsString()
	@IsNotEmpty()
	GITHUB_CLIENT_SECRET: string;

	@IsString()
	@IsNotEmpty()
	GITHUB_CALLBACK_URL: string;

	@IsString()
	@IsNotEmpty()
	GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID: string;

	@IsString()
	@IsNotEmpty()
	GOOGLE_CLIENT_SECRET: string;

	@IsString()
	@IsNotEmpty()
	GOOGLE_CALLBACK_URL: string;

	@IsString()
	@IsNotEmpty()
	FRONTEND_URL: string;

	@IsString()
	@Length(64, 64)
	ENCRYPTION_KEY: string;
}

export function validate({
	envConfig,
}: {
	envConfig: Record<string, unknown>;
}): EnvironmentVariables {
	const validatedConfig = plainToInstance(EnvironmentVariables, envConfig, {
		enableImplicitConversion: true,
	});

	const errors = validateSync(validatedConfig, {
		skipMissingProperties: false,
		forbidUnknownValues: true,
		stopAtFirstError: true,
		forbidNonWhitelisted: true,
	});

	if (errors.length > 0) {
		Logger.error('Config validation error', errors);
		throw new Error(errors.toString());
	}

	return validatedConfig;
}
```

1	import { Inject, Injectable, Logger } from '@nestjs/common';
2	import { Redis } from 'ioredis';
3	import Keyv from 'keyv';
4	import Redlock, { Lock } from 'redlock';
5	
6	import {
7		CACHE_READ_MANAGER,
8		CACHE_WRITE_MANAGER,
9		REDIS_READ_CLIENT,
10		REDIS_WRITE_CLIENT,
11	} from '@modules/cache/cache.constant';
12	import {
13		CacheError,
14		ConsistencyLevel,
15		Namespaces,
16	} from '@modules/cache/types/cache.interface';
17	
18	export { ConsistencyLevel, CacheError } from '@modules/cache/types/cache.interface';
19	
20	@Injectable()
21	export class CacheService {
22		private readonly logger = new Logger(CacheService.name);
23		private readonly redlock: Redlock;
24		private readonly redisWriteClient: Redis;
25		private readonly redisReadClient: Redis;
26		private readonly cacheWriteManager: Keyv;
27		private readonly cacheReadManager: Keyv;
28		private readonly recentWrites = new Map<string, number>();
29		private readonly CONSISTENCY_WINDOW_MS = 1000;
30		private isReadReplicaHealthy = true;
31	
32		constructor(
33			@Inject(CACHE_WRITE_MANAGER) cacheWriteManager: Keyv,
34			@Inject(CACHE_READ_MANAGER) cacheReadManager: Keyv,
35			@Inject(REDIS_WRITE_CLIENT) redisWriteClient: Redis,
36			@Inject(REDIS_READ_CLIENT) redisReadClient: Redis,
37		) {
38			this.cacheWriteManager = cacheWriteManager;
39			this.cacheReadManager = cacheReadManager;
40			this.redisWriteClient = redisWriteClient;
41			this.redisReadClient = redisReadClient;
42			this.redlock = new Redlock([redisWriteClient], {
43				driftFactor: 0.01,
44				retryCount: 10,
45				retryDelay: 200,
46				retryJitter: 200,
47			});
48	
49			this.setupHealthChecks();
50			this.startRecentWritesCleanup();
51		}
52	
53		async get<T>({
54			namespace,
55			key,
56			consistencyLevel = ConsistencyLevel.EVENTUAL,
57		}: {
58			namespace: Namespaces;
59			key: string;
60			consistencyLevel?: ConsistencyLevel;
61		}): Promise<T | null> {
62			const namespacedKey = this.getNamespacedKey({ namespace, key });
63	
64			const readFromMaster = this.shouldReadFromMaster({
65				namespace,
66				key: namespacedKey,
67				consistencyLevel,
68			});
69			const cacheManager = readFromMaster ? this.cacheWriteManager : this.cacheReadManager;
70			const source = readFromMaster ? 'master' : 'replica';
71	
72			try {
73				const result = (await cacheManager.get(namespacedKey)) as T;
74	
75				if (!result) {
76					this.logger.log('Cache miss', { namespace, key, consistencyLevel, source });
77					return null;
78				}
79	
80				this.logger.log('Cache hit', { namespace, key });
81	
82				return result;
83			} catch (error) {
84				const e = error as Error;
85				this.logger.error(`Error retrieving from cache: ${e.message}`, e.stack, { source });
86	
87				if (!readFromMaster && this.isReadReplicaHealthy) {
88					this.logger.warn('Read replica failed, falling back to master', {
89						namespace,
90						key,
91					});
92					this.isReadReplicaHealthy = false;
93					return this.get({ namespace, key, consistencyLevel: ConsistencyLevel.STRONG });
94				}
95	
96				throw this.createCacheError({ code: 'CONNECTION_FAILED', originalError: e });
97			}
98		}
99	
100		async set({
101			namespace,
102			key,
103			value,
104			ttl,
105		}: {
106			namespace: Namespaces;
107			key: string;
108			value: unknown;
109			ttl?: number;
110		}): Promise<void> {
111			const namespacedKey = this.getNamespacedKey({ namespace, key });
112	
113			try {
114				await this.cacheWriteManager.set(namespacedKey, value, ttl);
115				this.trackRecentWrite({ key: namespacedKey });
116				this.logger.log('Cached value', { namespace, key, ttl });
117			} catch (error) {
118				const e = error as Error;
119				this.logger.error(`Error setting cache: ${e.message}`, e.stack);
120				throw this.createCacheError({ code: 'OPERATION_FAILED', originalError: e });
121			}
122		}
123	
124		async has({
125			namespace,
126			key,
127		}: {
128			namespace: Namespaces;
129			key: string;
130		}): Promise<boolean> {
131			this.logger.log('Checking cache key existence', { namespace, key });
132			const value = await this.get<unknown>({ namespace, key });
133			return value !== null;
134		}
135	
136		async deleteOne({
137			namespace,
138			key,
139		}: {
140			namespace: Namespaces;
141			key: string;
142		}): Promise<boolean> {
143			try {
144				const namespacedKey = this.getNamespacedKey({ namespace, key });
145				await this.cacheWriteManager.delete(namespacedKey);
146				this.logger.log('Deleted cache key', { namespace, key });
147				return true;
148			} catch (error) {
149				const e = error as Error;
150				this.logger.error(`Error deleting from cache: ${e.message}`, e.stack);
151				return false;
152			}
153		}
154	
155		async deleteAll({ namespace }: { namespace: Namespaces }): Promise<boolean> {
156			try {
157				await this.cacheWriteManager.clear();
158	
159				await new Promise((resolve) => setTimeout(resolve, 100));
160	
161				await this.cacheReadManager.clear();
162				this.recentWrites.clear();
163				this.logger.log('Cleared all cache', { namespace });
164				return true;
165			} catch (error) {
166				const e = error as Error;
167				this.logger.error(`Error clearing cache: ${e.message}`, e.stack);
168				return false;
169			}
170		}
171	
172		async incrementByOne({
173			namespace,
174			key,
175		}: {
176			namespace: Namespaces;
177			key: string;
178		}): Promise<void> {
179			try {
180				const namespacedKey = this.getNamespacedKey({ namespace, key });
181				await this.redisWriteClient.incr(namespacedKey);
182			} catch (error) {
183				const e = error as Error;
184				this.logger.error(`Error incrementing cache key: ${e.message}`, e.stack, {
185					namespace,
186					key,
187				});
188				throw error;
189			}
190		}
191	
192		async withLock<T>({
193			lockKey,
194			operation,
195			ttl = 5000,
196		}: {
197			lockKey: string;
198			operation: () => Promise<T>;
199			ttl?: number;
200		}): Promise<T> {
201			this.logger.log('Acquiring lock', { lockKey, ttlMs: ttl });
202			const lock = await this.redlock.acquire([lockKey], ttl);
203	
204			try {
205				return await operation();
206			} finally {
207				this.logger.log('Releasing lock', { lockKey });
208				await lock.release();
209			}
210		}
211	
212		async acquireLock({ lockKey, ttl }: { lockKey: string; ttl: number }): Promise<Lock> {
213			try {
214				this.logger.log('Acquiring lock', { lockKey, ttlMs: ttl });
215				return await this.redlock.acquire([lockKey], ttl);
216			} catch (error) {
217				this.logger.error(`Failed to acquire lock: ${error}`);
218				throw error;
219			}
220		}
221	
222		async releaseLock({ lock }: { lock: Lock }): Promise<void> {
223			try {
224				this.logger.log('Releasing lock', { lockKey: lock.resources.join(',') });
225				await lock.release();
226			} catch (error) {
227				this.logger.error(`Failed to release lock: ${error}`);
228				throw error;
229			}
230		}
231	
232		private getNamespacedKey({
233			namespace,
234			key,
235		}: {
236			namespace: Namespaces;
237			key: string;
238		}): string {
239			return `${namespace}:${key}`;
240		}
241	
242		private shouldReadFromMaster({
243			key,
244			consistencyLevel,
245		}: {
246			namespace: Namespaces;
247			key: string;
248			consistencyLevel: ConsistencyLevel;
249		}): boolean {
250			if (consistencyLevel === ConsistencyLevel.STRONG) {
251				return true;
252			}
253	
254			if (!this.isReadReplicaHealthy) {
255				return true;
256			}
257	
258			const lastWriteTime = this.recentWrites.get(key);
259			if (lastWriteTime) {
260				const timeSinceWrite = Date.now() - lastWriteTime;
261				return timeSinceWrite < this.CONSISTENCY_WINDOW_MS;
262			}
263	
264			return false;
265		}
266	
267		private trackRecentWrite({ key }: { key: string }): void {
268			this.recentWrites.set(key, Date.now());
269		}
270	
271		private startRecentWritesCleanup(): void {
272			setInterval(() => {
273				const now = Date.now();
274				for (const [key, timestamp] of this.recentWrites.entries()) {
275					if (now - timestamp > this.CONSISTENCY_WINDOW_MS * 2) {
276						this.recentWrites.delete(key);
277					}
278				}
279			}, this.CONSISTENCY_WINDOW_MS).unref();
280		}
281	
282		private setupHealthChecks(): void {
283			this.redisReadClient.on('error', (error) => {
284				this.logger.error('Read replica connection error:', error);
285				this.isReadReplicaHealthy = false;
286			});
287	
288			this.redisReadClient.on('ready', () => {
289				this.logger.log('Read replica connection restored');
290				this.isReadReplicaHealthy = true;
291			});
292	
293			this.redisWriteClient.on('error', (error) => {
294				this.logger.error('Write client connection error:', error);
295			});
296		}
297	
298		private createCacheError({
299			code,
300			originalError,
301		}: {
302			code: CacheError['code'];
303			originalError?: Error;
304		}): CacheError {
305			const error = new Error(`Cache operation failed: ${code}`) as CacheError;
306			error.code = code;
307			if (originalError) {
308				error.originalError = originalError;
309			}
310			return error;
311		}
312	}
313

```
import * as fs from 'node:fs';
import * as path from 'node:path';

import { ConfigFactory } from '@nestjs/config';
import * as dotenv from 'dotenv';

import { validate } from '@config/env-validations.config';

import { isDevEnv, isProdEnv, isStagingEnv, isTestEnv } from '@utils/common.util';

export const getEnvFile = (): string => {
	if (isTestEnv()) return '.env.test';
	return '.env';
};

export const getEnvVariables = (): Record<string, string | number | undefined> => ({
	NODE_ENV: process.env['NODE_ENV'],
	PORT: process.env['PORT'],
	GLOBAL_PREFIX: process.env['GLOBAL_PREFIX'],
	SERVER_NAME: process.env['SERVER_NAME'],
	IS_COMPACT_LOGS: process.env['IS_COMPACT_LOGS'],
	IS_JSON_LIKE_LOGS: process.env['IS_JSON_LIKE_LOGS'],
	GRACEFUL_SHUTDOWN_DELAY: process.env['GRACEFUL_SHUTDOWN_DELAY'],
	DATABASE_URL: process.env['DATABASE_URL'],
	DATABASE_URL_READ_REPLICA: process.env['DATABASE_URL_READ_REPLICA'],
	REDIS_URL: process.env['REDIS_URL'],
	REDIS_READ_URL: process.env['REDIS_READ_URL'],
	JWT_SECRET: process.env['JWT_SECRET'],
	JWT_ACCESS_TOKEN_EXPIRY: process.env['JWT_ACCESS_TOKEN_EXPIRY'],
	JWT_REFRESH_TOKEN_EXPIRY_DAYS: process.env['JWT_REFRESH_TOKEN_EXPIRY_DAYS'],
	GITHUB_CLIENT_ID: process.env['GITHUB_CLIENT_ID'],
	GITHUB_CLIENT_SECRET: process.env['GITHUB_CLIENT_SECRET'],
	GITHUB_CALLBACK_URL: process.env['GITHUB_CALLBACK_URL'],
	GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID: process.env['GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID'],
	GOOGLE_CLIENT_SECRET: process.env['GOOGLE_CLIENT_SECRET'],
	GOOGLE_CALLBACK_URL: process.env['GOOGLE_CALLBACK_URL'],
	FRONTEND_URL: process.env['FRONTEND_URL'],
	ENCRYPTION_KEY: process.env['ENCRYPTION_KEY'],
});

export const loadEnvVariables = (): ConfigFactory[] => {
	if (isStagingEnv() || isProdEnv()) {
		validate({ envConfig: getEnvVariables() });
		return [getEnvVariables];
	}

	const envFilePath = path.resolve(process.cwd(), getEnvFile());

	if (!fs.existsSync(envFilePath)) {
		if (isDevEnv()) {
			throw new Error(`Env file not found: ${envFilePath}`);
		}
		return [() => ({})];
	}

	const envConfig = dotenv.parse(fs.readFileSync(envFilePath));

	validate({ envConfig });

	return [() => envConfig];
};
```

export enum ConsistencyLevel {
	EVENTUAL = 'eventual',
	STRONG = 'strong',
}

export interface CacheError extends Error {
	code: 'CONNECTION_FAILED' | 'KEY_NOT_FOUND' | 'OPERATION_FAILED';
	originalError?: Error;
}

export enum Namespaces {
	SESSION = 'session',
	AUTH_CODE = 'auth-code',
	LINKING_TOKEN = 'linking-token',
	OAUTH_STATE = 'oauth-state',
}

import {
	CanActivate,
	ExecutionContext,
	ForbiddenException,
	Injectable,
} from '@nestjs/common';
import { Reflector } from '@nestjs/core';

import { ROLES_KEY } from '@modules/auth/decorators/roles.decorator';
import { IRequestUser } from '@modules/auth/types/auth.interface';

import type { FastifyRequest } from 'fastify';

@Injectable()
export class RolesGuard implements CanActivate {
	constructor(private readonly reflector: Reflector) {}

	canActivate(context: ExecutionContext): boolean {
		const requiredRoles = this.reflector.getAllAndOverride<('admin' | 'member')[]>(
			ROLES_KEY,
			[context.getHandler(), context.getClass()],
		);

		// eslint-disable-next-line @typescript-eslint/no-unnecessary-condition
		if (!requiredRoles || requiredRoles.length === 0) return true;

		const request = context
			.switchToHttp()
			.getRequest<FastifyRequest & { user?: IRequestUser }>();
		const user = request.user;

		if (!user) throw new ForbiddenException();
		if (!user.role) throw new ForbiddenException();
		if (!requiredRoles.includes(user.role)) throw new ForbiddenException();

		return true;
	}
}
```

import { SetMetadata } from '@nestjs/common';

export const ROLES_KEY = 'roles';

export const Roles = (...roles: ('admin' | 'member')[]): ReturnType<typeof SetMetadata> =>
	SetMetadata(ROLES_KEY, roles);
---
import { createParamDecorator, ExecutionContext } from '@nestjs/common';
import { FastifyRequest } from 'fastify';

import { IRequestUser } from '@modules/auth/types/auth.interface';

interface AuthenticatedRequest extends FastifyRequest {
	user: IRequestUser;
}

export const CurrentUser = createParamDecorator(
	(_data: unknown, ctx: ExecutionContext): IRequestUser => {
		const request = ctx.switchToHttp().getRequest<AuthenticatedRequest>();
		return request.user;
	},
);

  "content": "# GitHub App Integration Implementation Plan\n\n> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Implement `src/modules/github/github-app.service.ts`**\n\n```typescript\nimport { createSign } from 'node:crypto';\n\nimport { HttpService } from '@nestjs/axios';\nimport { Injectable, Logger } from '@nestjs/common';\n\nimport { CacheService, ConsistencyLevel } from '@modules/cache/cache.service';\nimport { Namespaces } from '@modules/cache/types/cache.interface';\nimport { EnvConfigService } from '@modules/env-config/env-config.service';\nimport { IGithubApiInstallation, IGithubApiInstallationToken, IGithubApiRepo, IGithubApiReposListResponse } from '@modules/github/types/github-api.interface';\n\nimport {\n\tGITHUB_APP_ID,\n\tGITHUB_APP_PRIVATE_KEY,\n} from '@constants/env-variables.constant';\n\nconst GITHUB_TOKEN_TTL_MS = 55 * 60 * 1000;\n\n@Injectable()\nexport class GithubAppService {\n\tprivate readonly logger = new Logger(GithubAppService.name);\n\n\tconstructor(\n\t\tprivate readonly configService: EnvConfigService,\n\t\tprivate readonly cacheService: CacheService,\n\t\tprivate readonly httpService: HttpService,\n\t) {}\n\n\tgenerateAppJwt(): string {\n\t\tconst appId = this.configService.get<string>({ key: GITHUB_APP_ID });\n\t\tconst rawKey = this.configService.get<string>({ key: GITHUB_APP_PRIVATE_KEY });\n\t\tconst privateKey = rawKey.replace(/\\\\n/g, '\\n');\n\n\t\tconst now = Math.floor(Date.now() / 1000);\n\t\tconst header = Buffer.from(JSON.stringify({ alg: 'RS256', typ: 'JWT' })).toString('base64url');\n\t\tconst payload = Buffer.from(\n\t\t\tJSON.stringify({ iat: now - 60, exp: now + 600, iss: appId }),\n\t\t).toString('base64url');\n\t\tconst data = `${header}.${payload}`;\n\n\t\tconst sign = createSign('RSA-SHA256');\n\t\tsign.update(data);\n\t\tconst signature = sign.sign(privateKey, 'base64url');\n\n\t\treturn `${data}.${signature}`;\n\t}\n\n\tasync getInstallationToken({ installationId }: { installationId: number }): Promise<string> {\n\t\tconst cached = await this.cacheService.get<string>({\n\t\t\tnamespace: Namespaces.GITHUB_TOKEN,\n\t\t\tkey: String(installationId),\n\t\t\tconsistencyLevel: ConsistencyLevel.STRONG,\n\t\t});\n\n\t\tif (cached) {\n\t\t\treturn cached;\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tconst jwt = this.generateAppJwt();\n\t\tconst { data } = await this.httpService.axiosRef.post<IGithubApiInstallationToken>(\n\t\t\t`https://api.github.com/app/installations/${installationId}/access_tokens`,\n\t\t\t{},\n\t\t\t{\n\t\t\t\theaders: {\n\t\t\t\t\tAuthorization: `Bearer ${jwt}`,\n\t\t\t\t\tAccept: 'application/vnd.github+json',\n\t\t\t\t\t'X-GitHub-Api-Version': '2022-11-28',\n\t\t\t\t},\n\t\t\t},\n\t\t);\n\n\t\tawait this.cacheService.set({\n\t\t\tnamespace: Namespaces.GITHUB_TOKEN,\n\t\t\tkey: String(installationId),\n\t\t\tvalue: data.token,\n\t\t\tttl: GITHUB_TOKEN_TTL_MS,\n\t\t});\n\n\t\tthis.logger.debug(`Generated installation token for installation ${installationId}`);\n\t\treturn data.token;\n\t}\n\n\tasync getInstallation({\n\t\tinstallationId,\n\t}: {\n\t\tinstallationId: number;\n\t}): Promise<IGithubApiInstallation> {\n\t\tconst jwt = this.generateAppJwt();\n\t\tconst { data } = await this.httpService.axiosRef.get<IGithubApiInstallation>(\n\t\t\t`https://api.github.com/app/installations/${installationId}`,\n\t\t\t{\n\t\t\t\theaders: {\n\t\t\t\t\tAuthorization: `Bearer ${jwt}`,\n\t\t\t\t\tAccept: 'application/vnd.github+json',\n\t\t\t\t\t'X-GitHub-Api-Version': '2022-11-28',\n\t\t\t\t},\n\t\t\t},\n\t\t);\n\n\t\treturn data;\n\t}\n\n\tasync ${companyId}`);\n\t}\n\n\tprivate async syncRepos({\n\t\tinstallationId,\n\t\tinstallationDbId,\n\t}: {\n\t\tinstallationId: number;\n\t\tinstallationDbId: string;\n\t}): Promise<void> {\n\t\ttry '@modules/github/types/github-api.interface';\nimport { GithubInstallationStatus } from '@modules/github/types/github-installation.interface';\n\nimport { GITHUB_APP_WEBHOOK_SECRET } from configService: EnvConfigService,\n\t\tprivate readonly githubInstallationModel: GithubInstallationModel,\n\t\t@InjectQueue('github') private readonly githubQueue: Queue,\n\t) {}\n\n\tverifySignature({\n\t\tpayload,\n\t\tsignature,\n\t}: {\n\t\tpayload: string;\n\t\tsignature: string;\n\t}): boolean {\n\t\tconst secret = this.configService.get<string>({ key: GITHUB_APP_WEBHOOK_SECRET });\n\t\tconst computed = `sha256=${createHmac('sha256', secret).update(payload).digest('hex')}`;\n\n\t\tconst trusted = Buffer.from(signature);\n\t\tconst untrusted = Buffer.from(computed);\n\n\t\tif (trusted.length !== untrusted.length) return false;\n\t\treturn timingSafeEqual(trusted, untrusted);\n\t}\n\n\tasync handleEvent({\n\t\tevent,\n\t\tinstallationId,\n\t\tpayload,\n\t}: {\n\t\tevent: string;\n\t\tinstallationId: number;\n\t\tpayload: unknown;\n\t}): Promise<void> {\n\t\tconst installation = await this.githubInstallationModel.findByInstallationId({\n\t\t\tinstallationId,\n\t\t});\n\n\t\tif (!installation) {\n\t\t\tthis.logger.warn(`Webhook received for unknown installation: ${installationId}`);\n\t\t\treturn;\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tif (installation.status === GithubInstallationStatus.UNINSTALLED) {\n\t\t\tthis.logger.warn(`Webhook received for uninstalled installation: ${installationId}`);\n\t\t\treturn;\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tif (event === 'installation') {\n\t\t\tawait this.handleInstallationLifecycle({\n\t\t\t\tinstallationId,\n\t\t\t\tpayload: payload as IGithubInstallationEvent,\n\t\t\t});\n\t\t\treturn;\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tif (installation.status === GithubInstallationStatus.SUSPENDED) {\n\t\t\tthis.logger.warn(`Dropping event for suspended installation: ${installationId}`);\n\t\t\treturn;\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tawait this.githubQueue.add('raw_event', {\n\t\t\tevent,\n\t\t\tinstallationId,\n\t\t\tpayload,\n\t\t\treceivedAt: new Date(),\n\t\t});\n\n\t\tthis.logger.debug(`Queued ${event} event for installation ${installationId}`);\n\t}\n\n\tprivate async handleInstallationLifecycle({\n\t\tinstallationId,\n\t\tpayload,\n\t}: {\n\t\tinstallationId: number;\n\t\tpayload: IGithubInstallationEvent;\n\t}): Promise<void> {\n\t\tif (payload.action === 'deleted') {\n\t\t\tawait this.githubInstallationModel.markUninstalled({ {\n\tController,\n\tDelete,\n\tGet,\n\tHttpCode,\n\tHttpStatus,\n\tParam,\n\tPost,\n\tQuery,\n} from '@nestjs/common';\nimport { ApiOperation, ApiResponse, ApiTags } from '@nestjs/swagger';\nimport { FastifyReply } from 'fastify';\n\nimport { CurrentUser } from '@modules/auth/decorators/current-user.decorator';\nimport { Roles } from '@modules/auth/decorators/roles.decorator';\nimport { IRequestUser } from '@modules/auth/types/auth.interface';\nimport { EnvConfigService } from '@modules/env-config/env-config.service';\nimport { GithubInstallService } from '@modules/github/github-install.service';\n\nimport { FRONTEND_URL } from '@constants/env-variables.constant';\n\nimport { Res } from '@nestjs/common';\nimport { LogExecution } from '@decorators/log-execution.decorator';\n\n@ApiTags('GitHub Integration')\n@Controller('integrations/github')\nexport class GithubInstallController {\n\tconstructor(\n\t\tprivate readonly githubInstallService: GithubInstallService,\n\t\tprivate readonly configService: EnvConfigService,\n\t) {}\n\n\t@Post('connect')\n\t@Roles('admin')\n\t@HttpCode(HttpStatus.OK)\n\t@ApiOperation({ summary: 'Initiate GitHub App installation' })\n\t@ApiResponse({ status: HttpStatus.OK, description: 'Returns GitHub install URL' })\n\t@LogExecution()\n\tasync connect(\n\t\t@CurrentUser() user: IRequestUser,\n\t): Promise<{ url: string }> {\n\t\tconst url = await this.githubInstallService.initiateConnect({\n\t\t\tcompanyId: user.company_id,\n\t\t\tuserId: user.user_id,\n\t\t});\n\t\treturn { url };\n\t}\n\n\t@Get('callback')\n\t@Roles('admin')\n\t@ApiOperation({ summary: 'GitHub App installation callback' })\n\t@ApiResponse({ status: HttpStatus.FOUND, description: 'Redirects to frontend' })\n\t@LogExecution()\n\tasync callback(\n\t\t@Query() query: { installation_id: string; state: string; setup_action: string },\n\t\t@Res() reply: FastifyReply,\n\t): Promise<void> {\n\t\tconst frontendUrl = this.configService.get<string>({ key: FRONTEND_URL });\n\n\t\ttry {\n\t\t\tawait this.githubInstallService.handleCallback({\n\t\t\t\tinstallationId: parseInt(query.installation_id, 10),\n\t\t\t\tstate: query.state,\n\t\t\t});\n\n\t\t\tawait reply.redirect(`${frontendUrl}/settings/integrations?connected=github`);\n\t\t} catch {\n\t\t\tawait reply.redirect(\n\t\t\t\t`${frontendUrl}/settings/integrations?error=github_connect_failed`,\n\t\t\t);\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\t@Delete('installations/:id')\n\t@Roles('admin')\n\t@HttpCode(HttpStatus.NO_CONTENT)\n\t@ApiOperation({ summary: 'Disconnect GitHub installation' })\n\t@ApiResponse({ status: HttpStatus.NO_CONTENT, description: 'Installation disconnected' })\n\t@LogExecution()\n\tasync disconnect(\n\t\t@Param() params: { id: string },\n\t\t@CurrentUser() user: IRequestUser,\n\t): Promise<void> {\n\t\tawait this.githubInstallService.disconnect({\n\t\t\tinstallationDbId: params.id,\n\t\t\tcompanyId: user.company_id,\n\t\t});\n\t}\n}\n```\n\n- [ ] **Step 5: Implement `src/modules/github/github-webhook.controller.ts`**\n\n```typescript\nimport {\n\tBody,\n\tController,\n\tHeaders,\n\tHttpCode,\n\tHttpStatus,\n\tPost,\n\tUnauthorizedException,\n} from '@nestjs/common';\nimport { ApiOperation, ApiResponse, ApiTags } from '@nestjs/swagger';\n\nimport { Public } from '@modules/auth/decorators/public.decorator';\nimport { GithubWebhookService } from '@modules/github/github-webhook.service';\n\nimport { LogExecution } from '@decorators/log-execution.decorator';\n\n@ApiTags('GitHub Webhooks')\n@Controller('integrations/github')\nexport class GithubWebhookController {\n\tconstructor(private readonly githubWebhookService: GithubWebhookService) {}\n\n\t@Post('webhook')\n\t@Public()\n\t@HttpCode(HttpStatus.OK)\n\t@ApiOperation({ summary: 'Receive GitHub App webhook events' })\n\t@ApiResponse({ status: HttpStatus.OK, description: 'Event received' })\n\t@ApiResponse({ status: HttpStatus.UNAUTHORIZED, description: 'Invalid signature' })\n\t@LogExecution()\n\tasync handleWebhook(\n\t\t@Headers() headers: Record<string, string>,\n\t\t@Body() rawBody: string,\n\t): Promise<void> {\n\t\tconst signature = headers['x-hub-signature-256'] ?? '';\n\t\tconst event = headers['x-github-event'] ?? '';\n\t\tconst installationIdRaw = headers['x-github-hook-installation-target-id'] ?? '0';\n\n\t\tconst isValid = this.githubWebhookService.verifySignature({\n\t\t\tpayload: rawBody,\n\t\t\tsignature,\n\t\t});\n\n\t\tif (!isValid) {\n\t\t\tthrow new UnauthorizedException('Invalid webhook signature');\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tconst installationId = parseInt(installationIdRaw, 10);\n\t\tconst payload = JSON.parse(rawBody) as unknown;\n\n\t\tawait this.githubWebhookService.handleEvent({ event, installationId, payload });\n\t}\n}\n```\n\n- [ ] **Step 6: Run tests to confirm they pass**\n\n```bash\ncd backend && npm run test:unit --
